MIWSTRY OF PERSONI-JP.L, P.G, & PENSIONS 

(Department of Personnel & Training) 

New Delhi, ihe 10th September, 1986 

S.O, 3280.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 and clause (5) of article 148 of the 
Constitution and after consultation with the Comptroller and 
Auditor General of India in so far us persons serving in the 
Indian Audit and Accounts Department are concerned, the 
President hereby makes the following rules further to amend 
the Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, namely :— 

1. (1) There rules may bo called the Central Civil Services 
(Conduct) Second Admendrnent Rules, 1986. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publi- 
calioh in the Official Gazette. 

2, In the Central Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, 
for rule 15, the following rule shall bo substituted, namely:—< 

‘15. private trade of employment:— 

(1) Subject to the provisions of sub-rule (2), no Govern¬ 
ment servant shall, except with the provisions san¬ 
ction of the Government.— 

(a) engage directly or indirectly in any trade or business, 

or 

(b) negotiate for, or undertake, any oher employment, or 

(c) hold an elective office, or canvass for a candidate 

or candidates for an elective office, in any body, 
whether incorporated or not, or 

(d) canvass in support of any business of insurance 
agoncy, commission agency etc. owned or managed 
any member of his family, or 

(e) Take part except in the discharge of his official 

duties, in the registration, promotion or manage¬ 
ment of any bank or other company registered, or 
required to be registered, under the Companies Act, 
1956 (1 of 1956), or any other law for the time 
being in force, or of any co-operative society for 
Commercial purpose. 

(a) undertake honorary work of a social or charitable 
sanction of the Government.— 

(a) Undertake honorary woilc of a social or charitable 
nature, or 



3797 


[qrn II—'ai>5r 3 (ii) ] 27, 198 e/^nfif^'i s, i a o s 


<b) undertake occasional work of a literary, artistic or 
scientific character, or 

(c) participate in sports activities as an amateur, or 

(d) take part in the registration, promotion or manage- 
mcnl (not involving the holding an elective office) 
of a literary, scicniiflc or charitable society or of a 
club or similar organisation, the aims or 
objects of which relate to promotion of spoils, 
cultural or recreation activities, registered under the 
Societies Registration Act, I860 (21 of 1860), or any 
other law lor the time being in force, or 

(c) take part in the registration, promotion or manage¬ 
ment (not involving the holding of elective office) 
of a co-operative society substantially for the benefit 
of Government servants, registered under the Co¬ 
operative Societies Act, 1912 (2 of 1912) or any 
other law for the time being in force: 

Provided that 

(i: he shall discontinue taking part In such activities, if 
so directed by the Government; and 

(iij in a case falling under clause (d) or clause (c) of 
this sub-rule, his official duties shall not suffer thereby 
and he shall, within a period of one month of his 
taking part in such activity, report to the Govern¬ 
ment giving details of the nature of his parti¬ 
cipation. 

(3) Every Government servant shall report to the Govern¬ 
ment if any member of his family is engaged in a trade or 
business or owns or manages an insurance agency or com¬ 
mission agency. 

(4) Unless otherwise provided by general or special orders 
of the Government, no Government .servant may accept any 
fee for any work done by him for any private or public 
body or any private person without the sanction of the pre¬ 
scribed authority. 

Explnmlion.—The term 'fee'used here shall have tbe mean¬ 
ing assigned to it in Fundamenbil Rule 9(6-A). 

[No. 11013116|85-Estt. (A)] 
A. JAYARAMAN, Director

S.O. 3300.—Whereas, it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for 
the transport of petroleum from North Santhal to South 
Sonthat CTF in Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by 
the Oil & Natural Gas Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in 
that land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Goveinment 
hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of user 
therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object 
to the laying of the pipeline under the said land to the 
Competent Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, 
Construction and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, 
Vadodara, {390(X)9), 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
stale spciJificaTly whether he wishes to be hear in person or 
by legal practitioner.

S.O. 3301 .—Whereas by notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum & Natural Gas 
S.O. 2290 dated 28-5-86 under sub-scction (1) of Section 
3 of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Aequi.sition of 
Right of User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962). the 
Central Government declared its intention to acqjiie the 
right of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended 
to that notification foj- the purpose of laying pipeline. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub- 
Section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the 
Central Government hereby declares that the right of user 
in the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exercise of power conferred hy sub 
section (4) of the section, the Centr,al Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vest¬ 
ing in Central Government vests on this date of the publi¬ 
cation of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Com¬ 
mission free fron? encumbrances;

New Delhi, the 9th September, 19S6 

S,0. 3304.—Whereas by notification of the Qoverntnent of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas S.O. 
2282 dated 28-5-86 under sub-scclion (1) of Section 3 of 
the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right 
of User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central 
Government declared its intention to acquire the right of 
User in the lands specified in the schedule appended to that 
notification for the purpose of laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub- 
Section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified In the schedule appended to this noti¬ 
fication hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exercise of power conferred by sub-scction 
(4) of that section, the Central Government direct that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
Central Government vests on this date of the publication 
of this declaration in the Oil and Natural Gas Commission 
free from encumbrances.

to the Government;

considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the land^ specified In the schedule appended to this 
notification;

section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central

said lands specified In the schedule appended to this notifi¬ 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipeline;

free from encumbrances.

S.O. 3308.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the trans¬ 
port of petroleum from North Santhal to South Santhal CTF 
in Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil and 
Natural Gas Commission. 

And whereas It appears that for the purpose of laying such, 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the (Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 

Provided that any person Interested In the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the said land to the Com¬ 
petent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Con¬ 
struction and Maintenance DlGslon, Makarpura Road, 
Vadodara. (390009). 

And ever)' person makL"’g such an objection shall also 
State specifically whether he wishes to be hear In person or 
by legal practitioner. 



[Paht II— Sec. 3 (ii)l

S.O. 3309.—Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Miniatry of Petroleum and Natural Gas S.O. 
2117 dated 13'5'86 under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the 
Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of 
User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Govern¬ 
ment declared its intention to acquire the right of user in 
the lands specified in the schedule appended to that notifi¬ 
cation for the purpose of laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub- 
Section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act. submitted report to 
the Govertment; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the fight to 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notificBtionj 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified In the schedule appended to this notifi¬ 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that section, the Central Government directs that the 
rivht of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
Central Government vests on this date of the publication of 
this declaration in the Oil and Natural Gas Commission free 
from encumbrances.

S.O. 3310,—Whereas it appears to the Central Governnsent 
that it is necessary In the public interest that for the trans¬ 
port of petroleum from G.G.S. Ill to G.G.S. VI in Gujarat 
State pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 
Commission. 

And ■whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of User in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto: 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the said land to the Com¬ 
petent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Con¬ 
struction and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, 
Vadodara (390009). 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be hear in person or 
by legal practitioner.

S.O, 3311.—^Whereas by a notification of Government of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Ga* S.O. 
2454 dated 5lh July, 1986 under Sub-section (1) of Section 3 
of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right 
of User in Land) Act, 1962 fJO of 1962) the Central Govern¬ 
ment declared its intention to acquire the Right of User 
In the Lands specified in the schedule appended to that 
notification for the purpose of laying pipeline. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act submitted report to 
the Government. 

And further the Central Government has after considering 
the said report, decided to acquire the right of user in thq 
Lands specified in the schedule appended to this notification. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by Sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby declares that the right of user in the said lands 
specified in the schedule appended to this notification arc 
hereby acquired for laying the pipelines. 

And further, in exercise of the power conferred by Sub¬ 
section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of iwer in the said lands shall instead of 
vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of the 
publication of Jhis declaration in the Hindustan Petroleum 
Co)p, Ltd, Bombay free from aU encumbrances.

S.O. 3312,—^Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it i* necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from Hajira-Bareily to Jagdlshpur in 
Uttar Pradesh State Pipeline should be laid by the Gas 
Authority of India Ltd. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
■uch pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 


Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipefines (Acquisition of Right of User In the 
Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government 
hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of user 
therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Gas Authority of India Ltd. H, B. J. Pipeline 
Project B-581B. Aliganj Lucknow-226020 U.P. 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state speciflearfy whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by legal practitioner.

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
New Delhi, the 27th September, 1986 

S.O. 3329.—In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
17 of the Export (Quality Coutrol and Inspection) A.'t, 1963 
(22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby makes the 
following rules, namely :— 

1. Short title and commencement.—(1) These rules may 
be called the Export Inspection Council Contributory Provi¬ 
dent Fund Rules, 1986. 

(2) They shall come into force on tlie date of their pub¬ 
lication in the Official Gazette. 

2. Definitions.—In these i ulc.s, unless the context other 
wise requires:— 

(a) ‘Agency’ means the Export Inspection Agencies 
established under section 7 of the Act at Bombay, 
Calcutta, Cochin, Delhi and Madras ; 

(b) ‘Chairman’ means the Chairman of the Export Ins¬ 

pection Council; 

fc) ‘Council’ means the Export Inspection Council estab¬ 
lished under section 3 of the Act; 

(d) 'Director' means the Director of Inspection and Qua¬ 
lity Control of the Council; 

(e) “emoluments” means pay, officiating pay, special pay, 
personal pay, dearness pay or any other amount 
specially declared as such and which count for 
determining retirement benefits. leave salary, or sub- 
eistance grant as defined in the Fundamental Rules, 
Dearness Allowance and Additional Dearness Allow¬ 
ance include any wages paid by the Council or 
Agency to its employees not remunerated by fixed 
monthly pay;

S.O. 3330.—^In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
17 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection Act, 1963 
(22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby makes the 
following rules further to amend the Export (Quality Control 
and Inspection) Rules, 1964, namely:— 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Export (Quality Con¬ 
trol and Inspection) Amendment Rules, 1986. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publi¬ 
cation in the Official Gazette. 

2. In rule 3 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) 
Rules, 1964, in sub-rule (1), for the words “one year”, the 
words ‘two years" shall be substituted.

CANCELLATION ORDER 

(Office of the It. Chief Controller of Imports and Exports) 
Calcutta, the 28ih February, 1986 
CANCELLATION ORDER 

S,0. 3331.—^Whereas M|s. Hindustan Fertilizer Corporation 
Ltd. Durgapur Unit, Durgapur-713212, were granted a CG 
licence No. 1/CXJ/20837281C|XX|90|C|83 dt. 27-^84 for 
import of Capital goods. 


2. Whereas the firm have now filed an affidavit in terms 
of para 86 of the Hand Book, of Import Export Procedures 
1985—88 wherein they have stated that the Customs Pur¬ 
poses Copy of licence No. I/CG/2083728|C dt, 27-2-84 has 
been lost [misplaced after having been registered 'with Collec¬ 
tor of Customs, Customs House, Calcutta and not utilised at 
all. They have further undertaken to return the original 
Customs Purposes Copy of the said licence is found/lraced 
out at a later date, without utilisation, to this licensing office. 

3. Whereas the firm have since requested for issue of a 
duplicate Customs Purposes Copy only in cancellation of the 
Customs Purposes Copy of original CG licence No. I/CG/ 
2083728|C dt. 27-2-84 for utilisation for the full value of 
the licence. 

4. 1 am satisfied that the Customs Purposes Copy of the 
above mentioned licence has been lost/misplaced without 
having been utilised at all. 

5. In e.xcrcise of the power conferred on me under clause 
9(l)(d) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955, as amended, 
I hereby cancel the said original customs purposes copy of 
the licence No. 1/CG/20837281c dt. 27-2-84 and hereby 
Order that a duplicate licence be issued against the said can¬ 
celled licence. 

6. This is done in accordance with the provision of para 
86 'of the current Hand Book of Import-Export Procedures 
for 1985—88.

CANCELLATION ORDER 


S.O. 3332.—Whereas Mis. Sakbry Engineering Corpn. 
Jhargram, Distt. Miclnapore, W. Bengal were granted and AU 
licence No. PA>/2230178|CiXXi95lC|84 dt. 11-4-85 for 
Rs. 1,44,23,500 for import of CR Steel Sheets in Coils .5 MM. 
and thicker. 

2. Whereas the firm have now filed an Affidavit in terms 
of para 86 of the Hand Book of Import Export Procedures 
1985-86 wherein they have stated that both custom.s I'ur- 
poses Copy and Exchange Control Copy of the licence No, 
P/D/2230178|C-dt. 11-4-85 has been lostlimsplaced without 
having been registered with any customs authority and uti¬ 
lised at all. They have further undertaken to return the 
original licence (both Customs Purposes and Exchange Con¬ 
trol Purposes Copy) if found/traced out at a later date, 
without utilisation to this licensing office. 

3. Whereas the firm have since requested for issue of a 
duplicate Customs Exchange Control Purposes Copy only 
in cancellation of the said licence No, P/D/2230178|C dt, 
11-4-85 for utilisation of the full value of the licence. 

4. I am satisfied that both the Customs Purposes and 
Exchange Control Purposes Copy of the above mentioned 


licence have been lok/misplaccd without having been utilised 
at bU, 

5. In exercise of the power conferred in me under clause 
9(l)(d) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955, as amended, 
1 hereby cancel the said original Customs and Exchange 
Control Purposes Copy of the AU licence No. P/D/2230178fc 
dt. 11-4-85 and hereby order that a duplicate licence be 
issued against the said cancelled licence. 

6. This is done in accordance with the provision of para 
86 of the Hand Book of Import-Export Procedures, 1985-88.

without having been registered with any Customs Authority 
and utilised at all. They have further undertaken to return

New Delhi, the 10th September, 1986 

S. O. 3343,—Whereas Messrs Madras Fertilizers Limited, 
Madras 6000S8 (TN/7929) (hereinafter refemed to as the 
said establishment) have applied for exemption under sub¬ 
section (2A) of Section 17 of the Employees^ Provident Funds 


and Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (herein¬ 
after referred to as the said Act); 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution of payment of premium, in enjoyment 
ot benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India 
in the nature of Life Insurance which arc more favourable 
to such employees than the benefits admissible under the 
Employees Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 (herein¬ 
after referred to as the said Scheme) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of Section' 17 of the said Act and in conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Government of India in 
the Ministiy of Labour, S.O. 3665 dated the 19-7-1983 and 
subject to the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed 
hereto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of Iho 
said Scheme for a further period of three years with efiect 
from 24-9-1986 upto and inclusive of the 23-9-1989. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund Com¬ 
missioner, Tamil Nadu and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection as the Central Government 
may direct from time to time. 

2. The employer shall pay such inspection charges as the 
Central Government may, Irom time to lime, direct under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3A) of Section 17 of the said Act 
wltliin 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the 
Group Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, 
submission of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer 
of accounts, payment of inspection charges etc. shall be 
home by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rubs of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majority of the employees. 

5. Whereas, an employee, who is already a member of 
the Employees' Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a 
member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay necessary 
premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Corporation 
of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance 
Scheme appropriately, if the Iwnefits available to the em¬ 
ployees under the said Scheme arc enhanced so that the 
benefits available under the Group Insurance Scheme are 
more favourable to the employees than the benefits admissible 
under the said Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything conf.iined- in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, If on the death of an employee the amonnt 
payable under this scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the 
legal heir/nominec of the employee as compensation. 

8 . No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner. Tamil Nadu and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adversely the 
interest of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Com¬ 
missioner shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain tbair point of view. 

9. Where, for any reason, the employees of the said estab¬ 
lishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as 
already adopted by the sa’d establishment, or the benefits 
to the employees under, this Scheme are reduced in any 
manner, the exemption shall be liable to he cancelled 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to pay the 
premium etc. Within the due date, as fixed by the Life 



trratwTiinfl:t^rawrt 27 , lase/mfl^nr.s i908 


3885 


[htttII— 3(ii)] 

Insurance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled, 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payinent of premium the responsibility for payment of 
ass\irance benefits to the nominees or tlic legal heirs of 
deceased members who would have been covered under the 
said Scheme but for grant of this exemption, shall be that of 
the employer. 

12. Upon the death of the members cove'ej under the 
Scheme the Lite Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum assured to the nominee]legal 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any 
case within one month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

S.O. 3 344.Whereas Messrs Shri Ram Bearing Limited, 
34 Shakespears Sarani, Calcutta-700071 (WB111415) (hcein- 
after referred to as the said establishment) have applied for 
exemption under sub-section (2A) of Section 17 of the 
Employees’ Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provision* 
Act. 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to as the said 
Act). 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution of payment of premium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India 
in the nature of Life Insurance which are more favourable 
to such employees than the benefits admissible under the 
Employees Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 (herein¬ 
after referred to as the said Scheme); 

Now, therefore. In exercise of the powers conferred by 
tub-section (2A) of Secton 17 of the said Act and in Conti- 



3886 THE GAZETTE OF INOIA : SEPTEMBER 27.198«/ASVINA 5,1908 


[Part II— Sec. 3 (ii)] 


nualion of the notification of the Governtliont of India in 
the Ministry of Labour, S.O. 3654 dated the 25'7-1983 and 
subject to the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed 
hereto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme for a further period of three years with effect 
from 24-9-1986 upto and inctusive of the 23-9-1989, 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund Com- 
mis.uoner, West Bengal and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection as the Central Government 
may direct from time to time. 

2. The employer shall pay such inspection charges as the 
Central Government may, trom time to time, diiect under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3A) of Section 17 of the sad Act 
within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the 
Group Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, 
submission of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer 
of accounts, payment of inspection charges etc. shall be 
borne by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majority of the employees. 

5. Whereas, an employees, who is already a member of 
the Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a 
member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay necessary 
premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Corporation 
of India. 

6. The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance 
Scheme appropriately, if the benefits available to the em¬ 
ployees under the said Scheme are enhanced so that the 
benefits available under the Group Insurance Scheme are 
more favourable to the employees than the benefits admissible 
under the said Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, If on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this scheme bo less than the amount (hat 
Would be payable had employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the 
legal heir|nominee of the employee as compensation. 

8. No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commlss oncr, West Bengal and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adversely (he 
interest of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Com- 
misfioner shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Where,'for any reason, the employees of the said estab¬ 
lishment do not remain covered under the Group Insuraw* 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of Ind a as 
already adopted by the said establishment, or the benehts 
to the employees under this Scheme are reduced in any 
manner, the exemption shall bo liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to pay the 
premium etc. within the due date, as fixed by the Life 
Insurance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payment of premium the respon.sibllity for payment of 
assurance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of 
deceased members who would have been covered under the 
said Scheme ljut for grant of this exemption, shall be that of 
the employer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corooratlon of India shall ensure 

rompt payment of the sum assured to the nomlncellegal 
airs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any 


case within one month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

S.O. 3345.—^Wlureas Messrs J. V. Electronic Limited, 
808-9, Meghdoot, 90 Nehru Place, New Delhi-110019 (DL| 
3832) (hereinafter referred to ns the said establishment) have 
applied for exemption under sub-section (2A) of Section 17 
of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Pro¬ 
visions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to as the 
said Act). 

And whereas, the Central Government Is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution of payment of premium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of Ind'a 
in the nature of Life Insurance which are more favourable 
to such employees than the benefits admissible urder the 
Employees Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 (here'n- 
after referred to as the said Scheme) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred bv 
sub section (2A) of Section 17 of the said Act and in conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Government of Ind'a in 
the Ministry of Labour, S.O. 1896 dated the 30 3-1983 and 
subject to the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed 
hereto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme for a further period of three veprs with efllect 
from 6-4-1986 upto and inclusive of the 5-4-1989. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit .such returns to the Ernioual Prov'dent Fund Com¬ 
missioner, Delhi and mnintain «uch accounts and

2. The emnloyer shall pay such inspection charnes as the 
Central Government mav. fernn time to time, direct under

within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of tile 
Group Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounh. 


iubmission of returns, paympnt of iiisurancc premia, transfer 
of accounts, payment of inspection charges etc. shall be 
borne by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majority of the employees. 

5. Whereas, an employees, who is already a member of 
the Employees' Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a 
member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay necessary 
premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Corporation 
of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance 
Scheme appropriately, if the benefits available to tlie em¬ 
ployees under the said Scheme are enhanced, so that the 
benefits available under the Group Insurance Scheme arc 
more favourable to the employees than the benefits admissible 
under the said Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amo'mt 
payable under this scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the 
legal hcir|nominee of the employee as compensation. 

8 . No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior anproval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Delhi ard 
where any amendment is likely to alTect adversely the 
interest of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Com¬ 
missioner shall before giving his approval, give a rea'Onable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view, 

9. Where, for any reasoti, the en.ployees of the said estab- 
Hshment do not remain covered under the Group Insurai ce 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of Iiid'a as 
already adopted by the said establishment, or the btnsfi's 
to the employees under this Scheme are reduced in any 
manner, the exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to nay the 
premium etc. within the due date, as fl-xed by the’Life 
Insurance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payment of premium the re.snonsibility for payment of 
arsurance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of 
deceased members who would have been covered under the 
said Scheme but for grant of this exemption, shall be that ot 
the employer,

Scheme the Life Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum assured to the nomiueellegnl 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any 
case within one month from the receipt of claim complete m 
all respects.

S.O. 3346.—Wh'Sreas Mea.srs. The Periyar District Co¬ 
operative Spinnine Mills Limited, Post Box No. 8, Dharpu- 
rBm-638656 (TN|5544) (hereinafter referred to as the said 
establishment) have applied for exemption under sub-section 
(2A) of Section 17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds and 
Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter 
referred to as the said Act). 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution of payment of premium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Croup Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India 
In the nature of Life Insurance which are more favourable 
to such employees than the benefits admi.ssible under the 
Employees Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 (herein¬ 
after referred to as the said Scheme); 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of Secton 17 of the said Act and in Conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Government of Ind'a in 
(he Miniitry of Labour, S.O. 2320. dated (he 6-.5-19R3 and 
subject to the conditions .ipecified in the Schedule annexed 
hereto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme for a further oc-iod of three years with effect 
from 21-5-1986 upto and inclusive of the 20-3-1989. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund Com¬ 
missioner, Tamil Nadu and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection as the Central Government 
may direct from time to time. 

2. The employer shall pay such inspection charges as the 
Central Government may, from time to time, direct under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3.\) of Section 17 of the said Act 
within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. AU expenses Involved in the administration of the 
Group Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, 
submission of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer 
of accounts, payment of inspection charges etc. shall be 
borne by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, nlongwith a translation of the salient features 
thereof. In the language of the majority of (he employees. 

5. Whereas, an employees, who is already a member of 
the Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment, the employer shall immedialelv enrol him as a 
member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay necessary 
premium in respect of him to the Life In.surance Corporation 
of India. 

6. The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance 
Scheme appropriately, if the benefits available to the cm- 







[vm II—3(ii) ] 


27, 1986/WTftjpr S, 1908 


3889 


ployees under the said Scheme are enhanced so that the 
benefits available under the Group Insurance Scheme are 
more favourable to the employees than the benefits admissible 
under the said Scheme. 

7, Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the 
legal heir|nomince of the employee as compensation, 

8, No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Tamil Nadu and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adversely Ihe 
interest of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Com- 
shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable opportunity 
to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9, Where, for any reason, the employees of the said estab¬ 
lishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India ns 
already adopted by the said estnbli.shment, or the benefits 
to the employees under this Scheme are reduced in any 
manner, the exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer falls to pay the 
premium etc. Within the due date, as fixed by the Life 
Insurance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payment of premium the responsibility for payment of 
a.ssurance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of 
deceased members who would have been covered under the 
said Scheme but for grant of this exemption, shall he that of 
the employer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corpnmtinp of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum assured to the nominecllcsal 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for if and in any 
case within one month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

(hereinafter referred to as the said Aet,) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that 
the employees of the said establishment are^ without making 
any separate contribution or payment of preniiunij in etyoy- 
ment of bonellts under the Group Insurance Scheme of the 
Life Insurance Corporation of India in the nature of Life 
fnsurance which are more favourable ■ to such employees 
that the benefits admissible under the Employees' Deposit 
Linked Insurance Scheme. 1976 (hereinafter referred to the 
said Scheme). 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of section 17 of the said Act and subject 
to the conditions specified in the Schedule armexed hereto, 
the Central Government hereby exempts the said establish¬ 
ment from the operation of all the provisions of the said 
Scheme for a period of three years. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment 
shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner, Poona and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection, as the Central Govern¬ 
ment may direct from time to time.

Central Government may, from time to time, direct under 
clause (a) of sUb-soction (3A) of section 17 of the said Act, 
within 15 days from the close of every month, 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the 
Group Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accoimts, 
submission of return.s. payment of insurance premia, transfer 
of accounts, payment of inspection charges etc. shall be borne 
by the employer. 


4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishment, a copy of the rules of the Croup Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as

teuturcs thereof, in the language of the majority of the 
employees. 

5. Whereas an employee, who is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident Eund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment exempted under the said Act. is employed in 
his c^tabiishmenL the employer shall immediately enrol him 
as a member of the Group insurance Scheme and pay neces¬ 
sary premium in respect of him to the Eife Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance Scheme 
appropriately, if the benefits available to the employees 
under the said Scheme are enhanced, so that the benefits 
avlaiblc under the Gioup Insurance Scheme are more favour¬ 
able to the employees than the benefits admissible under the 
said Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount

would be payable hud employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference lo the 
legal heir/nominee of Ihe employee as compensation. 

8. No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of 
the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Poona and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adveisely the interest 
of the employees, the Regional Pjovident Fund Commis¬ 
sioner shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Where, for any reason, the employees of the said esta¬ 
blishment do not icmain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as already

S.O. 3350.—Whereas Messrs Industrial Linings Limited, 
425 Pgnthion Road, Egmore, Madras-600008 (TN/6993) 

(hereinafter referred to as the said establishment) have 
applied for exe,mplion under sub-section (2A) of Section 17 
of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Pro¬ 
visions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to at 
the said Act) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that 
the cinpfoyecs of the said establishment arc, without making 
any separate contribution or payment of premium, in enjoy¬ 
ment of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the 
Life Insurance Scheme of the Life Insmancc Corporation of 
India in the nature of Life Insurance which are more favour¬ 
able to such employees than the benefits admissible under 
the Employees Deposit Linked Insiuancc Scheme^ 1976 
(hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme). 

Now. therefore, in exercise of (he powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of Section 17 of the .aid Act and in conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Government of India in 
the Ministry of Labour, S.O, 2792 dated the I3-/-U,;.2 ,l-I 
subject to the conditions specified in ihc Scncjulc ai.iic.'.^J 
hereto the Central Government hereby e.xempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of 
the said Scheme for a further period of three years with 
elTect from 31-7-1985 upto and inclusive of the 30-7-1988. 

SCHEDULE 

1. Tlie employer in reliition to the said establishment 
shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner, Tamil Nadu and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection, ns the Central Govern¬ 
ment may direct from time to time. 

2. The employer shall pay such Insnectlon charges as the 
Cenlial Government may, from time to time, direct under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3A) of section 17 of the said Act, 
within 15 days frojn the close of every month.

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the 
Group Insmance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, 
submission of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer 
cf accounts, pavment of inspection char, 2 es etc. shall be borne 
by the employer. 

4. 'pie employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishment, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, as approved by the Central Government amj, u.s 
and when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient 
features thereof, in the language of the majority of the 
employees. 

5. Whereas an employee, who is already g member of the 
Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
estaJblisliment, the employer shall immediately enrol him 
as a member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay neces¬ 
sary premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance Scheme 
appropriately, if the benefits available to the employees 
under the said Scheme arc enhanced so that the benefits 
(tsailable under the Group Insurance Scheme are more favour¬ 
able to the employees than the benefits admissible under the 
said Scheme. 

7. Nolwithst.anding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this Scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable bad employee been covered under the 
said Scheme, the employer shall pav the difference .to the 
legal heir/nominee of the employee as compensation. 

B. No amendment of the provi.sions of the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of 
the Regional Provident 'Fimd Commissioner Tamil Nadu and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adversely the interest 
of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Contmis- 
sioner shall before giving his approval, -give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Vl'Tterc. for any reason the employees of the said esta¬ 
blishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
.Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as already 
adopted bv the said cstablisliment, or the benefits to the 
employees under this Scheme are reduced .in any ffcanner, 
the exemption shall be liable to he cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to pay the 
premium etc. within the due date, as fixed by the Life Insu¬ 
rance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to lapse, 
the exemption >s liable to be cancelled. 

II In case of default, if any made by the employer 
in paym.ent of premium the responfibility for payment of 
assurance benefits to the nominees or the lej;al heirs of 
deceased members who would have been covered under the 
said Scheme hut for grant of this exemption, shall be that 
of the employer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum assured to the nominee/Iegnl 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any case 
within one month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

S.O. 3351.—Whereas Messrs The Premier Automabilies 
I-im.'lct1, LBS Mari', Kurla, BombBy-400070(MH/74') (herein¬ 
after icferred to as the said establishment) have applied for 
exemption under sub-section (2A3 of Section 17 of the 
Employees’ Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions 
2 \ct, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to as the 
said Act) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that 
the employees of the said establishment are^ without .making 
any separate contribution or payment of premium, in enjoy¬ 
ment of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the 
Life Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of 
India in the nature of Life Insurance which arc more favour¬ 
able to such employees than the benefi's adrnissiblc under 
the Employees Deposit l.inked Insuiancs Scheme, 1976

hereto the Central Government hereby exemnts the said

employees.

under the said Scheme arc enhanced, so that the benefits

AWARD 

Datod August 29, 19W 

This is a reference made by the Oovernmeot of India 
in the Ministry of Labour for adjudication of the following 
dispute vide Notification No. L-220ll(41)|83-D, in(B)lD 
(V) dated 26th AprU. 19«4:— 

"Whether the action of the management of WCL, 
Pench Area in relation to their Eklehra Colliery 
in terminating the services of Shrl Harihar S|o 
Shyamrao, DPR. with effect from 13*3-1978 is 
Justified? If not to what relief it the workman 
•ntitledP’ 

2. In view of my order dated 10th February 1986 which 
is Annexure A of this award. I gave an opportunity to the 
parties to adduce evidence and prove or disprove the mis¬ 
conduct before me. Parties stated that they have already 
adduced Evidence in this regard and they rely on the tame. 
1, therefore, proceed to examine whether misconduct Is 
proved or not. in the light of the following issues framed 
by my ieanjed predecessor. 

ISSUES 

1. Whether the actioit of the management of WCL 

Pench Area in relation to their Eklehra Colliery 
in terminating the services of Shri Harihar S|o 
Shyamrao D.P-R. with effect from 13-3-1978 is 
justifiied 7 

2. If not, relief and costs ? 

FINDINGS WITH REASONS: 

3. Management ha.s examined Uma Shankar Singh (MWl) 
who proved the notice of termination dated 17*3-1978 (Ex. 
Mil). His statement i* that the workman Harihar absented 
himself from duty from 2-11-1977 without giving applica¬ 
tion or intimation. Therefore Ws services were terminated vide 
notice dated 17-3-1978 (Ex. Mjl) In his cross^xamination 
he stated that notice Ex. Mil was sent by registered A.D. 
to the workman on 27-3-1978 as per the despatch register. 
It appears that the A.D. receipt has not been received back. 
He has, however, stated that it was only after about four 
years of termination that the workman came and wanted 
reinstatement. The prosecution hat also relied bn the copies 
register of leave account for the veais 1977*78 and the 
bonus register Ex, M|3 for the same years. 

4.. In rebuttal workman Harihar examined himself alone. 
Before I take up his statement it will be useful to mention 
what were his pleadings in this regard. His case was that he 
had fallen sick and was under treatment of outside doctors 
with effect from November 1977 to April 1978 at Ms home 
place. He was on leave and came with medical certificate 
and submitted the same on 25-4-1978 bnt the management 
had terminated his ser^cc well in advance from 13-3-1978 
without notice and eoqniry. Now firstly he states thgt in the 
winter 1977 he had gone home where ho got Malam which 
fnmed into Tvphoid and he was ill for about three years. 
This belies his very pleading that he was only ill from 
November 1977 to April 1978 l.e. about five months but 
now be says that he was ill for about three years. Next he 
says that he was treated privately so he did not bring any 
medical certificate. This is also contrary to his pleading that he 
gave and submitted hfs medical certificate on 25-4-1978. 
But he again turned turtle' an a leading question and stated 
that he had brou^t the medical certificate and had given 
H to the Manager, Shr) Kalian, and on his asking he had 
left the same on hli table. Next in his cross-examination he 
stated that he had gone on seven days leave after It was 
sanctioned but before the expiry of leave he fell HI, There¬ 
fore he had applied for further leave for two weeks. This was 
neither pleaded nor anv evidence adduced to prove the 
leave appHestion. His statement clearly goes to show that 
ho is changing the stand from time to time. As such he Is 
not felfing the truth. In fact as the management has adduced 
evfdence. it ancftars the workman had absented himself with¬ 
out leave from 2-11-1977 which amounts to misconduct 
tsdthin the meaning of Clause fn) Sulvclnnse (1) of Clause 
is of the Stanfing Ordars. 


5. In the circumstances I find that the termination of the 
workman, Shri Harihar Slo Sui Shyamrao, DPR with effect 
from 13-3-1978 is justified. In view of the period of his ab¬ 
sence and his conduct punishment awarded appears to be 
also just and proper. The workman concerned is not entitled 
to any relief or costs of these proceedings. Issues are 
decided accordingly and the reference is answered aa 
under :— 

That the action of the management of WCL, Pench Area 
in relation to their Eklehra Colliery in terminating the ser¬ 
vices of Shri Harihar CIo Shyamrao, D.P.R. with effect from 
13-3-1978 is justified and he is not entitled to any relief. No 
drder at to coata. 

V. S. YADAV, Presiding Officer. 
ANNEXURE ‘A’

Employers in relation to the management of Eklehra

ORDER 

Dated February 10th, 1986 

This is a reference made by the Oovt. of Intlia in the 
Ministry of Labour for adjudication of the following dispute 
vide Notification No. L-2201I(41)|83D.in(B)|D.(V) dated 
26th April, 1984:— 

"Whether the action of the management of WCL, 
Pench Area in relation to their Eklehra Colliery 
in terminating the services of Shri Harihar Slo 
Shyamrao, D.P R. with teffcct from 13-3-1978 is 
justified 7 If no), to what relief is the u'orkman 
enb'tled ?” 

2. Nou-cotrovursial facts of the case are that Shri Harihar 
waa permanent (Group) worker of D.P.R, (Gang) of Eklehra 
Colliery, He was absent from dutv from 2-1-1977 onwards, 
He services were therefore termiated with effect from 
13-3-1978 by wav of automatic termination under Clause 
19 of the Standing Orders. 

_ 3. The case of the applicant workman is that he had fallen 
sick and was under treatment by outside Medical Doctor 
from November 1977 to April 1978 at his home place. He 
was on leave and cam© with the Medical certificate to 
resume duties. He submitted his Medical Certificate but his 
servicea were already terminated. 

4. His servicet were thus terminated without any notice 
Or enqtHry, In similar case learned predecessor this Tribu¬ 
nal had set aside tbe order of termination in case No. 
CGITlLCfR) (26)11982. 

5. The case of the management is that the workman nei¬ 
ther sent any application for leave nor intimated his abwnce. 
Even after his termination he kept qu'et for six years. His 
•endees were terminated under Cl. 19 of the certified 
Standing Orders. In any case absent without leave constitute 
a misconduct. In mining industry absence causes serious 
difficulty in the industry. The management intimated the 
workmen regarding automitic termination of his rervice. 
Action of the management is justified as was held by the 



3902 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; SEPTEMBER 27,1986/ASVINA 5,1908 [Part II—S^C. 3 (ii)] 


Presidinu Officsr of ibii, Tiibunal io Case No. CGlTlLCCR) 
(9)179. 

6. The mana?:i]cnt has lelied on Ex M|1 notice of ter¬ 
mination dated Z3-1-78/]7-3-78, Register of leave Account 
of the workman (Ex. M/2), Bonus Register Ex. Ml3 and the 
staten^int of their employee Uma Shankar Singh (M.W.l). 
Uma Shanktr Singh has proved no.jce Ex. Mil and sta'ad 
that the workman neither sent ajiy intimation nor obtained 
leave. 

7. On the other hand, workman Harihar (W.W. 1) has 
staled that he fell ill and he was ill for three years. When 
he got alright he came to work but the management did 
not allow him to join duty. However contrgjw to this he 
stated that he had obtained leave before goii^ home and 
he had filed a Medical Certificate on return. But no such 
Medical Certificate or receipt etc. for the same is filed. I-Toin 
the above evidence, it is proved that he was appointed on 
12-1-74 (Ex. M|3) and thus he was a permanent’workman 
of the management within the meaning of Sub-Clause (5) 
of Clause 3 of the Standing Orders. It is fiiihe- proved that 
he had absented himself without leave or intimation from 
the aforesaid date. 

8. On behalf of the managcineni reliance is placed on 
Clause 19 and Sub-clause l(n) of Clause 18 of the Standing 
Orders. The first limb of its contention is that Clause 19 
reproduced below lays down that absence from duty without 
giving any information to the management for more, than 3(i 
days automatically temiinalas the .services of the workman 

“If a workman absents himself without giving any in¬ 
formation to the manager for more than 30 days 
his services will nufomatically stand terminated." 

Zhe second limb of ic contention is that Sub-Clause (iXn) 
Of Clause 18 clearly lay.s clown that “Continuous ab.sence 
without permission and without satisfactory cause for more 
than ten days amounts to misconduct. Therefore his services 
were terminated for misconduct vide order dated 13-1-78/ 
17-3-1978 (Ex. M/1). 

9. On behalf of the woikman the Union repiesentative has 
challenged the applicability of the said Standing Orders. I 
am of the opinion that I need not go thi'ough this aspect 
of the matter for which no proper foundation has been laid 
by the evidence and production of doeumerits. 

10. I have already pointed out that firstly it is proved 
that the workman Shri Harihar was a peimanent workman. 
Clause 14 of the Standing Order? lays down that for termi¬ 
nation of the services of permanent workman having less 
than one year continuous service as defined in Section 2(oo) 
of the I.D. Act, 1974 a notice in writing or wages in lieu 
Ihereof at the scale indicated below shall he given by the 
employer;— 

(I) For monthly paid workman ; One Aonth: 

(ii) For weekly paid workman : Two weeks. 

Provided no such notice is necessary if his services are 
terminated on account of misconduct. 

Admittedly no such notice or wages in lieu Ihereof has 
been given. To may toind the provision of Clause 19 arc 
■subject to the condition of Claiise 14 being fulfilled. That 
have not done this Clause 19 of the Certified Standing Order 
is of no avail to the mnnagomeut. 

11. Coming to the second limb of the contention. 1 find 
that oven if it amounts to m],iconduct,, even then his services 
could not have been terminated without notice and compen¬ 
sation. There is a proce(jure laid dovvn for disciplinary 
action for misconduct in Clause 18, the relevant portion of 
Sib-ciause (1) of Clause 18 is reproduced below for the sake 
of convenience:— 

“A workman may be suspended or fined or his Incre¬ 
ment may be stopped, or he may be demoted or 
dismissed without notice, if he is found to be guilty 
of misconduct." 

The same clause under Sub-dause (li) however lays down 
as under;— 


‘T8(li). No order of punishment under Standing Order 
No. 18(i) shall be made unles.s the workman con¬ 
cerned is informed in writing of the alleged mis- 
conclL-ct and is given an opportunity to explain the 
allegations made against hmi.' A departmental enquiry 
shall be instituted before dealing with the charges. 
During the period of enquiry, the workman concern¬ 
ed may be suspended. The workman may take the 
assistance of a co-worker to help him in the enquiry 
if he so desires. The pecoids of the departmental 
enquiry shall be kept in writing. The approval of 
the owner, or agent or the Chief Mining Engineer 
of the employer shall be obtained before imposing 
the punishment of dismissal.” 

Sub-clause (hi) and (iv) respectively lay down the procedure 
In case he is found guilty or not guilty. In this regard the 
contention of learned Counsel for the management is that 
dismissal for misconduct under Cl. (n) of Sub-cluiisc (1) of 
Clause 18 of the Certified- Standing Orders amounts to dis- 
mk.sal by disciplinary actiofi under Sec. 2(oo) of the I.D. 
Act therefore necessary show cara.se notice and disciplinary ac¬ 
tion by way of departmcnal enquiry was not necessary. This 
contention is without any substance, The word ‘disciplinary 
action' exempted under Section 2(oo) of the I.D. Act to my 
mind involves the disciplinary action commonly called the 
departmental enquiry. This plea is perhaps based on the pro¬ 
vision of Sub-rule (1) of Cl. 18 of the Standing Orders which 
says tliat even dismissal could be without notice but there 
are qualifying words preceding the words ‘aismissed without 
notice’ in case he is found gmliy of misconduct. The words 
‘found guilty’ suggests that there ought to be finding of mis¬ 
conduct by way of departmental enquiry before a person 
could be dismissed without notice for misconduct. Even 
under clause (h) of clause 9 it has been laid down as 
under :— 

“(h) Notwithstanding anyibing mentioned above, any 
workman who overst.tys his sanctioned leave or re¬ 
mains absent without reasonable cause will render 
himself liable for disciplinary action." 

Thus It is crystal clear that the Stttnding Orders envisages 
disciplinary action by way of departmental enquiry and 
rinding of guilt before services of permanent employee could 
be terminulcd without notice. It tluis cannot be said that 
terminatiou for misconduct under Clause 18 amounts to 
dismissal by disciplinary action under Section 2(oo) of the 
I.D; Act. 

12, Section 25-J provides “that the provisions of this Chap¬ 
ter shall have effect notwithstanding anything in any other 
law” including Sltlnding Orders made under the Industrial 
Employment (Standing Orders) Act, 1946 (XIX of 1946) 
(Provided that where under the provisions of any other Act 
or ruk-B, orders or notification issued thereunder or under any 
standing orders or under any award, contract of service or 
otherwise a workman is entitled to benefits in respect of any 
matter which are more favourable to him than those ' to 
which he would be entitled under this Act, the workman 
shall continue to be entitled to the moie favourable benefits 
in respect of that matter, notwithstanding that he receives 
benefits in respect of other matters under this Act). 

12) for the removal of doubts, it is hereby declared that 
nothing contained in this Chapter shall be deemed to affect 
provisions of any other law for the time being inforced in 
any State in so far as that law provide,? for the settlement 
of industrial disputes, but the rights and liabilities of emp¬ 
loyers and workmen in so far as they related to lay oS and 
retrenchment shall be determined in accordance with the 
provisions of this Chapter.’’ 

. 13. In the case qf Factory Manager C.I.M. Mfg. Cq. Ltd. 
Vs. Naresh Chandra fI985 Lab. I.C. p. 941) the High Court 
of Madhya Pradesh has observed as under,:— 

".in view of the catena of Supreme Court decisions 

termination of service under Standing Order 11 dlso 
will come within the mischief of relrtnchm’ent as 
defined under Section 2(oo> and unless S. 25F is 
complied with the termination will be void.” 

14. Both parties have also .relied on the Judgments of 
the predecessors of this Court. Respectfully I submit that the 
same arc not binding on this Tribunal, therefore I need not 
consider them. 




II—^ 3(ii)] "PT wm:fiP!P^2 7, 1 s8 6/wtfim^ 5, 19Q8_ 3903 


]5. Lastly on behalf of ihe managemcut, it; has beta 
cootended that in any case as pleaded in the ^vritten state¬ 
ment of claim the manaficment be given an opportunity to 
prove miscoildiict before ihiS Tribunal as lias been laid down 
in the workmen of Firestone Tyres & Rubber Company 
find tbe Management and others (1973-SClJ (10) 159) ; - ■ 

“ft there has been no enquiry held by the employer or 
if the enquiry is held to be defective, it is open to 
the employer even now to adduce evidence for the 
first time before the Tribunal justifying the order 
of discharge or dLsmissnl. We are not inclined to 
accept the contention on behalf of the workmen 
that the right of the employer to adduce evidence 
before the Tribunal for the first time recognised bjf 
this Court in its various decisions, has been taken 
away. There is no indication in the section that the 
said right has been abrogated. If the intention of 
the legislature was to do away with such a right, 
which has been recognised over a long period of 
years as will be noticed by the decisions referred to 
earlier, the section would have been differently 
worded. Admittcdlv there are no exnress words I hat 
effect and there is no indication that tbe section, 
has impliedly changed tbe law in that respect 
Therefore, the position is that even now the employer 
is entitled to adduce evidence for tbe first tiinc 
before the Tribunar even if he had bel l no enquiry 
or the enquiry held by him is found to be defective, 
of cour.so, an opportunitv will have to be given to 
the workman to lead evidence contra. 

16. In view of the above observations made by their 
Lordships of Suniemr Court I hold that the management is 
entitled to adduce evidence before this tribunal to prove the 
alleged misconduct and tbe Workman is entitled to rebut tbe 
same by evidence. 

17. It Is ordered accordingly. Parties are directed to produce 
their evidence on the date fixed.

khand' Copper Project of Hindustan Copper Ltd.

.Union; P.O. Malanikhand, Districi Balaghat tM.P.)

AWARD 

Dated ; August 28, 1986 

This is a reference made by the Central Government vide 
Notification No. L-43012|3[S4-D. III. B Dated 21st July, 1984 
lor adjudication of the following dispute;— 

“X^ffiether the action of the management of Malanjkhand 
Copper Project of Hindustan Copper Ltd., in strik¬ 
ing off (he name of Shri R. N. Sindhupc, Clcrk- 
cum-Typist from their rolls with effect from 20-3-83 
on his release from Jail, is justified? If not, to what 
relief is the workman concerned entitled?" 

2 . Hie material facts in this case arc not disputed. Ihc 
same are that Shri R.N. Sirulhupc was workirg as a Clerk- 
ciim-Typist in the office of lbs Hindustan Copper Ltd., 
Malanjkhand Project. He was absent from duty from 4-12-82 
till 11-1-1983 without obtaining prior leave. Subsequently 
on 11-1-19'83 be applied to the m.anaccmont that he was 
arrested by police and put in Jail for offence under Sec. 376 
I.P.C. On triel he was acquitted and relea.sed from Jail. On 
7 3-1983 he filed an application (Ex. Ml3) and on that appli¬ 
cation the management offered him appointment as a fresh 
candidate vide Ex. M|4 and he joined on 26-2-1983 vide 
joining report Ex. MI3, The workman raised the dispute 
before the A.L.C. (C) Chindwara through the Union and 
the mnnagement replied vide Fx. WU. However conciliation 
failed hence this reference. 

3. The case of the management further is that the work¬ 
man had neither applied for leave nor intimated about his 
being detained in Jail. Or 7-3-1983 the workman had prayed 
j,o tlic management that his services came to an automatic 
end due to his long absence when he v/ns detained in Jail 
and he has also lost his lien on his post. He be therefore 
given a fresh appointment. 

4. Tlie mn,nDgemcnt has taken legal, objbclions that the 
reference is bad. Firstly on the ground that the workman 
is in employment. As such there is no indu.strial dispute. 
•Secondly the Union Labour Leader who raised this dispute 
has no authority to do so. Thirdly tbe reference gives ffle 
wrong date of termination hence reference is bad in law. 

5. On the other hand, the case of the workman is that he 
had intimated the authorities about his being detained in 
Jail vide Fx. Wl2. Jnspite of hiG intimation management 
terminated his services. So when ho was released from Jail 
he opjrroached the authorities but they refused to reinstate 
him, Ultimateb' be was fold that unUss he eives m writing 
that be is willing for fresh appointment he will not be 
reinstated. 

6 . My learned predecessor framed the following Issues 
which with my reasons and findings are as under:—: 

ISSUES 

1. Whether the action of the manaaement of Mrilnnj- 

khand Copper Project of Hindustan Copper Ltd. 
in strike off the name of .Shr) R.N, Sindhnpe, Clerk- 
cum-Typist from their rolls w.e.f, 20-3-1983 on hia 
relen-e from Jail, is justified? 

2. Relief. 

Findings with Reasons ; 

7. Is.sue Nos, 1&2:—-Before J take up the issue on merit 
I would like to dispose of the legal objections first. Simply 

‘ he cause wrong date of termination has been mentioned it 
docs not make the reference bad in Ww. Secondly ff is a 
case of termination which is being challenged In thV. refe enco 
under Sec. 2A of the 1 D. Act. So it is Imn'jtcriaT. if he tivas 
'akon as a fresh apnointpietit .'Whether the Union leader con- 
L'erqed had .any anthority or not it is also imtUnterial 



3904 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; SEFlEMBER 27,1986/ASVlNA 5,1908 


[Part U—Sec. 3 (ii)] 


V. Coming to the merits of the ctuo it has beta contendod 
on behalf of the management that the workman himaelf had 
requested that he may be given a fresh appointment. There¬ 
fore he in now eatopped from raising thi* dlgpute. The work¬ 
man gave his statement on oath ann stated that he had to 
take fresh appointment since he was told that he will b* 
given appointment only if he gives in writing that he accepts 
fresh appointment. In support of his contention he has filed 
a copy of the application (Er, Wl3), Ea. M/3 ig the photo 
copy of the application dated 7-3-83 in which the words 
‘frosh-C-P-’ has been written in hand and the rest of the 
application in typo written. These words arc wanting in 
Ex, W/S. This goes to show that the word Tresh C-T’ were 
added subsequently perhaps os the workman says that he waa 
compelled to write that he accepts fresh appointment. On 
behalf of the management Dr. G. K. Mohanty has been 
examined. But he has not ihrotvn any light on the word 
‘Fresh C-T written in the application. I, therefore, see no 
reason to disbelieve the workman that he was compelled to 
seek fresh appointment. Therefore there is no question of 
his being estopped from challenging his termination. 

9. Next question arises whether in view of the provision 
of clause 19(e) of the Standing Orders and Clause 15,5 of the 
Settlement dated 23-5-1980 the workman is entitled to chal¬ 
lenge bis termination. It is true that the management has 
contended that his termination docs not amount to retrench¬ 
ment, As such provision of Section 25P of the I.D. Act ha.s 
no application os has been held in the case of Binny Ltd. Vs. 
Presiding Officer Labour Court (1986-I-LLI 237) which In 
ture had also relied on the pronouncement of the HonTile 
Supreme Court in the case of Buckingham and Kamatak Co. 
Ltd. Vs. Venkatayya (1963-11 LLJ 638), The services of the 
workman were dispensed with because as the management has 
stated that he had absented himself from duty without duo 
permission as laid down in Clause 19(e) of S. Orders and 
Clause 15.5. Annexure IX of Settlement dated 23-5-80, both 
reproduced below;— 

Clause 19(c) of Standing Orders ; 

“If a workman remains absent without permission or 
struck off from the rolls df'the Company unless he 
overstays the period of leave originally granted or 
subsequently extended he shall loose lien on hi* 
appointment and his name will automatically stand 
returns within ten days of the expiry of the leave 
Or the date from which he absented and explains 
to the satisfaction of the management his absence 
without permission or inability to return on the 
expiry of the leave." 

Clause 15.5 of the Agreement dated ^23-5-80 : 

"15.5. If a workman remains absent unauthorisedly for 
more than 10 days continuously he shall be deemed 
to have lost his lien on his appointment unless he— 

(a) returns within 10 days of the date of hitt losing 
the lien; end 

(b) emlains to the sati.sfaction of the Manpgement 

the reasons for unauthorised absence.” 

It is true that the above provisions provide for automatic 
termination of the service of the workman in cases of un¬ 
authorised absence for a particuler period. But it also lay* 
dowm that if ho returns and explain to the satisfaction of 
the management his services may not be terminated. The 
workman in this case ha* proved his application (^ W/2) 
sent from Jail dated 20-5-1982, This shows that he had 
intimated the authority about the cause of his absence. In 
.such circumstances the management ought to have consideibd 
that his explanation is sstisfac.'ory for his unauthorised absenoe 
Therefore the act of the management to treat his temti- 
natlon valid and give him fresh appointment does not appear 
to be justified and proper. 

10. As a last roilort, the management has contended that 
whether to reitwtMe tt give a freah appointment is a 
managerial function and It does not amount to industrial 
dispute. I am upable to agree. Section 2(00) of the I.D. Act 
while defining Vetfenchment’ exempts cases of disciplinary 
action from its purview. To dlS{)onse with the services of a 


workman under Clause 19(e) of the Standing Orders amounts 
to a disciplinary action in which no domestic eot^liy has 
been held as has been laid dowu in the case of M|s. Firestoiie 
Tyres & Rubber Factory Ltd.. (CIR 1973 SC 1227), Therefore 
this Court under Sec. 11-A of the I.D. Act can consider the 
propriety of the action taken as if it is. a case of dismissal 
where no domestic enquiry has been held. The management 
has not prayed in their written statement aeeking opportu¬ 
nity to prove misconduct before this Tribunal. Therefore 
firstly they are not entitled to the same and secondly iu any 
case parties have led evidence and from the evidence on 
record I find that the workman was confined in Jail and ho 
has Intimated this fact to the management. Therefore it does 
not amount to misconduct. In any case, his explanation 
should have been taken to be satisfactory. Therefore his 
termination is improper and bad in law. Consequently I 
answer the reference as under :— 

That the action of the management of Malanjkhand 
Copper Project of Hindustan Copper Ltd,, in strik¬ 
ing the name of Shri R.N. Sindhupe, Clerk-cum- 
Typist from their rolls with effect from 20-3-83 
(Correct date of termination is dated 20-8-82) on his 
release from Jail is not justified. Instead of being 
given a fresh appointment he should have been 
reinstated with effect from 20-8-1982 with full back 
wages and all other ancillary besefits and continuity 
of service. No order as to costa.

Whether the action of the managensmt of the State 
Hank of India in reation to its Kirauli Branch Agra 
in terminating the seiwiccs of Shri Sukhjit Singh, 
sub Staff, with effect from 27th June, 1982 is justi¬ 
fied? If not, to what relief is the workman con¬ 
cerned entitled?

permanent appointment in the management bank from

hence remains for seniority of the service. The case of the 
applicant is that he was appointed in the bank as guard

T.D, Act. In Ihe end d is averred that though he was ini- 
tiallv appointed on 22-11-80 he was fiv-'u artificial bleaks 
in order to IcpTve the workman to put in continuous 

'NSCTwice,

the workman were terminated as his services were no longer 
required in relieve arrangement and on that account none 
of the provisions of the law have been contravened.

ment and that he was initially absorbed on 27-3-85 hence his

2. The case of the workman is that he was appointed as
