some artificial breaks for about 157 days and he was paid 
bonus for the same. The services of tlic workman were 
lerniinaled abruptly without any notice or order on 1-1-82 
and on inquiry from thy management the workms^n was told 
to wait for some time, it is further averred that after the 
lentjination of the workman, management appornied some 
Ollier interested person in his place. The workroaD was 
neither given any appointment letter or termination letter 
as per rules, Tlte workman was also not given any notice 
at the ttne of termination of his services, thiis the manage¬ 
ment has coniravened the provsioirs of naturaf justice and 
provisions of bipartite seUletqents. It is further averred 
that the provision of para 493 Of the Sastri Award were not 
followed and the management appointed many other hand 
after the termination of workman’s service and thus the 
nianagerr,*eiit acted against the provisions of sections 25G 
and H of lire Industrial Dispute Act, 1947. It is further 
averred that the action of the management was illegal mala- 
iidc and amounts to victimisation. In the end the work¬ 
man has prayed that he Ire reinstated in service with full 
back wages, 

3. The management cuntesteri the petition of the work¬ 
man on the ground that the workman was given temporary 
appointment at its Firozabad Branch and the workman was 
engaged on daily wages and iifi such question of giving any 
appointment letter or icnijination letter is not arise. It is 
further averred that the workman was engaged on Rs. 8 
per day to lilf waler.s ip the coolers and drinking waters in 
the pilclrci's was was never asked to work as massengcr. It 
is further contended by the njanagsraent that the management 
appointed another person after his termination after ful- 
lilling the required norms and as the workman had put only 
157 days of service he is not entitled to the benefit of sec¬ 
tion 2SCt and 25H of the I.D, Act and that the services 
of the workman were not terminated mala fide and docs not 
amount to victimisation. The workirjap was engaged purelv 
on temporary basis for filling waters in the coolers oh the 
daily tvages. 

4. In the rejoinder, it is averred on behalf of the work¬ 

man were terminated with the intent to oblige some one 
CISC. . 

5. As the workman wanted production of certain docu¬ 
ments by the management it was ordered that let a joint 
inspection repent lx: i'dcU and the said joint inspection re¬ 
port i.s on record. From the said joint inspection report it 
appears that no attendance register is maintained for the 
temporaiy employees and that no appointment order or 
icrnftnalion order is issued In the case of temporary emp¬ 
loyees and no service hook was maintained and no service 
certificate was issued to workman and that after the ter¬ 
mination of the workman several persons were appointed. 

6 . The management has filed ll applications of the work¬ 
man fo,; the payment of Rs. 8 as wages for iillinc water 
and other misc. work. The workman has filed letter of the 
management hank from the personnel officer in winch it 
was specificully mentioned that though Ahduf Gaffar wort 
man W'as called for interview on 27-3-85 for recruitriKnt of 
massengcr cum guard and that the workman was called as 
direct candidates having served in the bank as temporary 
employee^ he was found unsuitable for appointment on per¬ 
manent basis. 'I'hiffl the management has admitted that the 
workman w:rs called for interview being a temporary emp¬ 
loyee. The biodata form shows that the work^n worked 
as messenger for 157 days w.e.f, 3^-81 to 31-12-81. 


was purchasing pitchers of water rather the case is that 
the work of fi.lling water and pitchers and other misc, work 
is taken from him as substantiated by letters of the work- 
ntsan filed, he was being paid Rs. 8 for his work. He fur¬ 
ther states that casual labours are appointed in the bank 
toi casual nature of work and that the caaual labours are 
not pad bonus though temporary employees are paid bonus, 
He further stated that he came to know from the records 
that the workman was working for 3 or 4 hours per day. 
It may be further mentioned here that no where the 
management took plea that the workman never worked full 
day rather the case is that he was paid Rs. 8 for filling 
water and other misc. work for the day. 

8 . On tile other hand the workman gave his affidavit. 
Workman has slated that he worked as peon in the bank 
and never worked as water boy for fill’ing pitcher etc. and 
giving drinking water to the customers and staff. He how¬ 
ever, admitted that he was paid Rs. 8 for that work. 

9. The whole question is whether the workman was work¬ 

ing in the bank os temporary workman or only a casual 
Workman for casual nature not connected with the 
banking industiy, liad there been any appointment letter 
that would have shown whether the workman was employ¬ 
ed for a spccifre period or for a specific work in which case 
empfoymenl .automatically comes to an emd on the expiry 
of such period or after such work. In the instant case 
employment was continued for 157 days from June to 
December 198], It can not be said that as the employ- 
ir/cnt was only for the summer season for Wling pitchers 
as he .was allowed to continue till 31st December 1981. 
Further the very fact that the workman was called for the 
interview treating him as temporary employee suggests that 
besides taking work of filling wafer in pitchers other tnJsc, 
work of sub staff was also taken from the workman during 
the banking hours. In the absence of any specific nature 
ol work onccmnectcd with, the banking industry, the only 
presumption is that he was engaged fOr misc. work of 
class IV'vtsuh staff) employee arising in the banking in¬ 
dustry. The workman has averred' that he worked as 
efass IV employee (massengcr) and it is not dispute as per 
joint inspection report that some euTployee were appointed 
after (he termination of tlie workman., if the temporary 
nature of work in llie banking industry was continuing he 
should not have been terminated or at the worst he should 
have been recalled and not the third person engaged, Non 
compliance of provision of section 25H of the ID Act and 
giving others appointment, will render his termination 

illegal, further non compliance of para 493 of the Sostri 
Award in not maintaining the seniority list of the tem¬ 
porary employees and further in not giving appointment to 
the workman and absence of appointment letter and termi¬ 
nation letter will also render termination illegal. In the 
written statement’s paragraph 2 the management has ad¬ 
mitted that the workman was given a temporary appoint¬ 
ment On 3rd lune which and continued til] December and 
he was paid boniLs. As the workman has acquired slatus 
of temporary workman his services should not hiive been 
brought to a end without giving birr, notice pay which was 
not in the instant case suggests that the management has 
contravened the provisions of paras of the Sastri Award 
and on this count to the termination of the workman ren¬ 
ders illegal termination. 

10. Thus for the reasons discussed above, I hold that the 
action of the management of State Bank of India, Region 
T in relation to its Firozabad Branch under the control of 
Regional manager, Agra, in terminating the services of Shri 
Abdul Gaffar sub staff from 31-12-81 is not justified and the 
■csiilt is that be is entitled to be reinstated in service with 
full l?ack wages.

AWARD 

1. The Central Government, Ministiy of Labour, vide Its 
notification No. L-12012/194/84-D. II (A) dt. 28-^5 has 
referred the following dispute for adjudicutiou to this tri¬ 
bunal; 

"Whether the action of the Zonal Manager Union Bank 
of India, Zonal Office, Lucknow In not paying 
Shri Ramjan Ali part time sweeper, Sagri Branch 
of the bank 1 /3rd authorised scale of the wages 
with effect from January, 1978 and not con¬ 
verting him into full time sweeper alongwith 
other in 1980-81 is justified and equitable? If not 
to what relief is the worljman is entitled?” 

2. It is common ground that the workman was appointed 
ns temporary part time sweeper on 15-3-72 and in due 
course his services were increased to l/3rd of scale vvages 
as his working hours in a week were not more than 12-3/4 
which he was getting as a part time permanent sweeper 
w.e.f, 23-11-79. It is further not disputed that the workman 
has been made full time sweeper from the year 1980-81. It 
is admitted that the bank’s premises was changed in 1973, 
the management witness did not remember the month but 
according to the workman the premises was changed on 
23-1-73 and .shifted to a new premises measuring about 1200 
sq, fit. It is further admitted that he was paid the same 
salary which he was paid for earlier pcrmiscs which accord¬ 
ing to the workman is Rs. 25 per month. His emoluments 
later increased from Rs, 25 to 50. It is further averred 
that till September 79 he was paid R.s, 50 per month when 
he is taken on permanent basis on payment of l/3rd wages. 
It is fiTrther averred on behalf of the workman that the 
manacement debarred the workman from getting facility 
of full time employee in view of staff circular no. 2188 dt. 
20th August, 1980 as he was entitled to become full time 
worker having studied upto 7th standard and having put 
in more than 7 ye.ars of service. The management further 
did not consider his conversion a.s a full time emjjloyeo 
in view of circular A-8462 of 82 dated 20-12-1982. 

3. The management in its written statement besides ad¬ 
mitted facts averred that when the workman anneared for 
conversion as full time workman in december 1981 he had 
not completed minimum 3 years service on 31-12-81 and 
therefore was not eligible for being taken up for full time 
subordinate. Tliat prior to Ist October, 79 the wonnan was 
not required to work for more than 6 hours per week and 


hcnco he liad no claijn foi- higher wages and that there K 
no rule or provision entitling woikman that his entue tem- 
XJOrarily ixinod of service be treated as temporary provision 
para 20.8 of the bipartite settlement arc not attracted on 
the basis of facts of the present case. 

4. In he irtcjoinder it is averred hat in view of para 20.S 
of the biparlric seLilcmcnt as the workman evciiiu.uly select¬ 
ed on the post on whrch he was workuii part Imic lie sltould 
be deemed confirmed after expiry ot six months of appomt- 
ment i.e. from 15-9-72 as he was appointed on 15-3-72. 

5. ill suppori of Its coalention the managemeril examined 
one onrr ouunir Misia the personnel onicer ol lire mange- 
fiieui nank wno gave his alhuavit evidence and deposed mat 
the workman was not appointed on 15o-V2 on any pc/maneni 
vaeancy ratner nc was engaged puicly on tempoiyny Oasis 
ill me newly opened oranen and Ijom 8-1U-84 he has oecomc 
lull iipic employee per appomimcnt teller wlucii is on re¬ 
cord. iic admits mat when the premises of the nank was 
cuangcd m 73 the carpet area or the picmiscs was about 
14U0 sq- tit and he was being paid the same saiaiy which he 
was paid earlier, but he was required to work 
for less than 6 hours a week, the workman 
was connrmed w.e.f. l-lU-79 on l/3rd pay but he was noi 
requned to work for more than 12-3/4 nouis a week. He 
has admitted that prior to 1-10-1979 the workman simply 
swept the premises but fiom 1-10-79 lie was required to 
clean furnitures and fixtures and performed other woik which is 
mentioned m me letter dt. 19th October 197y. copy or 
whicn IS filed. He lurlhcr stated that till December 1981 
ihc workman has not completed 3 yeais service hence he 
was not absorbed. 

6 . On the other hand workman has filed iris affidavit 
evidence- in cross examination he states that he use to 
obtain key of the branch from the branch manager, opened 
the premises and sweep the floor etc. and by 10.00 a.m. 
band over the key to the branch manager anti in this way 
he started working at 8 a.m. He further stated that he use 
to serve water to the officials of the branch. Fie adinns 
that he had no orders in writing about filling water and 
serving water to the officials and oply in Oct. 1979 a written 
order was given to him for that work. He depose that prior

no peon in the bank and use to woik for taking withdrawals 
to the cabin putting ihk in the pad. He admits that he never 
objected to those work on' the ground that he was not paid 
as peon, 

7. Temporary employee in the banking industry are full 
time employees working from Id a.m. to 5 p.m, as opposed 
to part tune employees who arc a different calcgory and 
arc required not to work for full day i.e. 10 to 5 p.m. but 
for a couple of hour for which wages arc paid to them. 
Thus para 20,8 apply only to temporary employees, there 
is no question of giving him benefit of past service when 
he was rtiade regular on 8th October, 1984. His services has 

, confirmed part time sweeper from 19th October, 1979 also 
also can not be taken into effect for reckoning his picviovra 
appointment for purposes of para 20,3 as even at that 
time ho was a part time employee. The woikmcn himself 
has admited that he use to work for two hours a day from 
' 8 am to 10 am, thus even calculating at this rate his work¬ 
ing hours in a week were not more than 12-3/4 hours as 
alleged by the management, hence workman would not be 
entitled to more than l/3rd scale rate wages from 1979 to 
onwards i.e. till his permanent appointment though his 
duties besides sweeping and cleaning furnitures were en¬ 
hanced to storing and supplying drinking waters. Prior to 
79 the workman was paid at the enhanced rale for working 
less than 6 hours in any week. Sweeping premises of 1000 
sq. fit will not take in any case more than one hours a day. 

8 . The management has averred every where that prior 
to 79 the workman never worked more than 6 hours in a 
week and on this point there is oath against oath and I 
am inclined to believe the pianagemcnt witness on the point. 

9. The result is that for a period prior (o October 1979 
the workman will not be entitled to one third authorise scale 
rale. I consequently hold that the action of the Zonal 
Manager Union Bank of India Zonal Office I.ncknow in 
not paying workman Sri Ramjan Ali part time sweeper 
sagri branch of the bank l/3rd of the authorise scale wages 
from 73 is justified. As regards not converting him into 
full time sweeper alongwith others in 1980-81 in view of 
circular dt. 20-8-80 annexure 3 of the workman's statement 





VTTOT wrr 27, ISSe/wifru-T 9, 1908 


3909 


uf. claim it is not disputed that the woikmuu was not 
a part lime sweeper may be (,n 25 or 50 jupccs per month 
from Jnnaury 1973 and has thus completed 7 years of ser¬ 
vice oil 20th ^ut'ust 1980. the w'oikman had applied for his 
convcision on 19-9-80 gtvinR reference to circular no. 218S 
dl. 21-8-80 be consideicd as full time sweeper as he was 
workinK as part lime since 1972 and hud passed 2th standard 
in 1971 which is Lmnexure 2 of the rejoinder of the work¬ 
man. The manaRement h;is not specifically denied that the 
workman had not passed 7lh class at the time of his appli¬ 
cation for conversion when on consideration of his appli¬ 
cation in December, 81 it was found that he had not com¬ 
pleted minimum 3 years service on 31-12-81. Annexure I of 
the workman’s afUdavit shows that he had applied for per 
manent appointment on 12th March, 1980, the management 
never challenged tliat the workman was not 7th class pass. 

10. In these circumstances tire management should have 
cosidered his application in 80 m view of circular no. 2188 
of 20th Au.cust, 1980 rmd as such he had completed as 
early on 13 ih September, 1980 he should have been con¬ 
sidered and made permanent from that date. I, consequent- 
l.v hold that the action of the management in not con¬ 
verting him into full time sweeper ulongwith others after 
sltiff circular no. 2188 of 21st August, 1980 and the appli¬ 
cant having domplction on 13 th September, 1980 is not 
justified. The result is that he will be deemed to be con¬ 
verted as full time sweeper w.e.f. 13th September, 1980 and 
will he cntilled to back w,iges from that date till he was 
made full lime on 8lh October, 1984. 

11. I. therefore, give my award accordingly.

ment for'its publication.

S.O, 3?70.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Oripiitcs Act. 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government

Industrial Tribunal No. 2, Dhanbad, as shown in the 
Annexurc. in the industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the management of D & F Ropewags, M|s. Bharat 
Coking Coal Ltd., Bhulnnhararee Camp, P.O. Patheidih, Distt. 
Dhanbad and their workmen,' which was received by the 
Central Government on the 2nd September, 1986.

WW-2 is one of the concerned workmen. He lias staled

The Coal Mines (Conservation and development! Act, 1974 
namely .Act No. 28 of 1974 was passed for the conservation 
of coal and development of Coal Mine.s and for matters 
connected therewith. Section 12 of the Act shows that on 
the appointed day the Coal Board, established under Section 
4 o£ the Coal Mines (Conservation, Safety and Development) 
Act. 1952 shall stand dis.solved and that on the di.ssolufion 
of the Coal Board all rights and privileges of the Coal Board 
■shall become the rights and privilegs, respectively of the 
Central Government. The Central Government shall be deem¬ 
ed to he the leroee of all propertic.s held by the Coal Board, 
immediately before the appointed day, under any lease and 
that Govemment shall hold the lease under the .same terms 
and conditions under which the lease was held by the Coal 
Board. Section 13 provides that the right, title and interest 
of fh,; Coal Board m relation to anv property shall, instead 
vrf continuing to vest in it, vest in the Government company 
cither on the date of publication of tjie direction or on such 
earlier or later dale as may be specified in the direction, and 
on such vesting, the. li&flffy or obligation, as the case may 
be. of the Coal Board In relatlbh to such prppepty shall, 
instead of continuing to be fhc liaffilify gp obugatign of, the 
Central Govemment, become the liability qt,. ObUgatkin, as 
the citec may be of the CovMTiment comphny Section 


15 deals with the transfer of service of existing employee* 
of Coal Board. It provides that cvejy officer or other emplo¬ 
yees of Coal Board shall become an officer or employee Of 
such Government conqvany or organisation as the (Central 
Government may. in \viitting speci^r aod shall hold its office 
or service in such Government company or organisation on the 
same terms and condition and that the same rights to pensiog, 
gratuity and other matters as would have been admissible to 
him if the Coal Board had not been absolished and shall con¬ 
tinue unless and until his empjoyment in the Governinent 
company is terminated or until his remuneration or terms and 
condition of service are duly altered by the Goverment com¬ 
pany. It further provided' that the tenure, remuneration and 
other terms and condition of service of such officers or emp¬ 
loyees shall not be altered to his disadvantage except With 
the previous approval of the Central Government. It will 
appear from the said Act that the terms and conditions of 
the Coal Itoard epiployses were protected and It appear* 
that some of *h* employees of the Coal Board who wore 
gciting the special pay might have continued to got fhe 
Special pay as their condition of sendee was piotectCKf, 99 
far the two concerned workmen are concerned it appear* 
that the concerned workmen Shri O. C, Binerjec wm nbt bn 
employee of Coal Board and Shri R. E- Das even 'f ho was 
an employee of Coal Board had opted for the pay scales of 
BCCL and as such his pay scale was fixed In the pay scale 
of the Wage Board Recommendation and NCWA. Volume 
II page 54 of the Wage Board Recommendation deals with 
the clerical staff grading and nomenclature. It shows that 
cashiers have been placed in Clerical Grade I and Asstt. 
Cashiers have been placed In Clerical Grade II. At page 
76 Clause 15 of Volume I of Wage Board Recommendatio* 
deals with the pay scales of the Clerical grades. The Wa^ 
Board Recommendation and NCWA I aial T1 aral III have 
not provided for any special pay for working as Cashier or 
Asstt. Cashier and their pay structure and other various 
duties were considered at the time when the Wage Board 
had fixed the scale of pay of the clerical grades. There hM 
been an enhancement of the scale of pay of the Clerical 
Grades but special pay has not been provided to cashier or 
.\sst(. Cashier for handling cash. It is clear therefore that no 
special pay is provided for handling cash as Cashier and 
Asstt. Cashier by the Wage Board Reaimmendation and 
Nf'W.As by which the employees o( BfCl are governed. 

Considering Ihc pay scale provided for the collieries under 
the Coni Wage Board Recommendation and NCWA’s and the 
de'-g iiion of rower.') to Ihc Managers vide Ext. W-4. 1 hoi,’ 
tli.'it Lxl. M-2 dated 26-4-80 issued witn the approval oF me 
Ci‘'ne>;il Manaccr ropcway.s had no authority to Si'Ticiion 
spc:ial pay of Its. 51)/- per month to tnc conrerned vorkmen 
and ns .such the concerned workmen cannot claim the special 
pay of K.s. 5P/- per month for handling cash. 

li is now adiuitted that the concerned workmen arg not 
handling cirsii. WW-1 Shri G. C. Bancrjec ha.s plated that 
since May, 1974 he is not handling ca,'h in the .opcv\ays 
and lhai Siu-i R, L. Das .ilso ii not hrindlimi c,i'-h since some¬ 
times before him. The order E,xt. M-2 shows that the special 
pny was allowed towards cash hanclUn.g. Since the concerned 
workmen were not handling cash since Mav, 1974 or some 
limes before it thrv cannot claim -'ven according tn p.xl, 
M-2 special pay of Rs. SO/- per month after May, 1974. 


The workmen have refeired to Ext. W-5 which is a miniite.s 
of discussion and it is submitted that the said Ext. W-5 
is a settlement. On the other hand it is submitted on behalf 
of Ihe management tha Ext. W-5 is only a minute of discus¬ 
sion and not a memorandum of settlement in accordance 
with the Rule 58 of the Industrial Disputes (Central) Rules, 
1957. On perusal of Ext. W-5 it appears th.tt it is not in 
accordance with Rule 58 of the Industrial Disputes Rules 
as it is not in Form-H. It is clear from the documents itself 
that Ext. W-5 is not a settlement and it is a mere minute of 
discussion showing that the management submitted before 
the concitiation officer that the amount of special pay of 
R.S. 50/- has been restored to, the workmen and ns such the 
demand was settled at that stage. It appears that the manage- 
met had pot given a notice under, Sutton 9(A) of the ID. Act 
before Stopping the,special pay of ^ 50/- to the conoertied 
workmen and as such the managHnent restored the ^teofal 



[m*! II—3(ii) 


IRTOT TT : ftdMIC 27, 198e/wTlW^T 5, 1908 


3915 


the conciliation officer. The manaKcment thereafter issued a 
jiotice Ext, M-1 under Section 9(A> of the l.D, Act and 
after oonsidering the representations from the ^vort* 
men stopped the payment of special pay of Rs, 30/- per 
month vide Ext. M-3 dated 1-4-83. It will appear from Ext. 
M-3 that the reasons have been given as to why the paytnent 
of special pay was being discontinued. The reasons given 
show that the authority who had sanctioned the special pay 
of Ks. 50/- to the concerned workmen vide Ext. M-2 had 
no authority to .sanction the said amount and as discussed 
above it will appear that the General Manager who had 
winctioned the amount vide Ext. M-2 had actually no authority 
to make sanction for the payment of special pay. 1 hold, 
therefore, that Ext. W-5 is only a minute of discussion and 
fa not a settlement as contemplated under Rule 58 of the 
l.D. Rules and nccotdingly It cannot be said that the present 
industrial dispute had been settled vide Ext. W-S. 

In the result, I hold that the action of the mbanagement 
« 0 & F Ropeways M/s. B.C.C. Ltd.. Bhnlanbararee pay 
' impt P.O. Patherdoh, Distt. Dhanbad in stopping the special 
jy of Rs. 50/- per month to Shri G.C. Banerjee, Cashier 
y Shri R.L. Das, Asstt. Cashier with effect from 1-4-83 is 
itifled and consequently the concerned workmen are entitled 
no relief. 

'Ihis is my Award.

AWARD 

The Govt, of India, Ministry of Labour in exercise of the 
powers conferred on them under Section 10(1) (d) of the 
1J>. Act., 1947' has referred the following dispute to this 
Tribuoai for adjudication vidPrtheir Oa^der No. L-20012(31)| 
85-D. ni (A), dated, the 29th May, 19B5. 


SCHEDULE 

“Whether the demand of Bihar Colliery Kamagar Union 
that the management of Benedih Colliery of Messrs. 
Bharat Coking Coal Limited should regularise their 
workman, Shri Santosh Bishnu as Overseer with 
payment of wages as Overseer with retrospective 
effect is justiBed? If so, to what relief is this work¬ 
man entitled and from what date?” 

In this case both the parties filed their respective W.S, 
documents etc. The workmen examined five witnesses and 
the case was fixed for evidence of the employers on 16-6-86. 
On that day both the parties prayed for time for filing 
settlement and ultimately on 6-3-86 both the parties appear 
before me and filed a memorandum Of settlement. 1 find that 
the terms of settlement are fair and proper, Accoiflingly 1 
accept the same and pass an Av/ard in terms of the memoran¬ 
dum of settlement which forms part of the Award as 
Annexure.

The case of the workmen, it appears, is not exactly for

cadre and not in the clerical cadre. The said objection appears 
to be quite frivolous. 

One of the obiection raised on behalf of the management 
is that the concerned workman had not made any demand

him to Grade-A. It is clear therefore that a demand was 
made on behalf of the concerned workman with the manage-

It will thus appear that theie are two groups of dependents 
provided uriaer clause (1) 10.4.2 of NCvVA-ii. I’he fUst 
group of dependents are wltihusband, umnarried daguhter, 
son and legally adopted son. If there is no such dhect 
tlepenoent of the deceased then there is another group of 
dependent provided and they are younger brother, widowed

dent on the earnings of the deceased. Maheshwar Pd. 
Mahato is the younger brother of deceased Kartik Mahato 
and as such his case falls within the second group of aepen- 
dent as Kartik Mahato had no direct dependent such as b' 
wire, unmarried daugher, son and legally adopted son. ' 
case of the first group of dependents who ar© direct ' 
dents of the deceased, the clause does not provide tha 
should be almost wholly dependent on the earnings 
deceased but in the case of second group of the de- 
of the deceased such as younger hi other etc. it is i 
that they should be residing with the deceased ar 
wholly dependent on the earnings of the deceased, 
therefore to see whether Maheshwar Pd. Mahato was 1 
along with late Kartik Mahato and was v/holly de| 
on the earnings of deceased Kartik Mahato.

AWARD 

Dated : August 18, 1986 

This is a reference made by the Central Oovermnent vide 
Notincation No. L-14012 (20jl85-D. iI(B) dated 24tb January, 
1986 for adjudication of the following dispute .— 

“Whether th© action of the management of Vehicle 
Pactopy, Jabalpur (iVl.P.) in terminating the ser¬ 
vices of Sri Mamiilal Sjo iPancham Singh, Labourer 
with e.cct from 15-10-84 is justified? If not, to what 
relief the workman concerned is entitled?” 

2. Facts in this case arc not disputed. Undisputed facts 
arc that the workman, Mannilal wag appointed in Lab. B in 
Vehicle Factory, Jabalpur vide order dated 7-9-1983 with 
eft«t from 19-9-1983 and hb services were terminated with 
efi'ect from 13-10-1984. Thus he had continuously worked 
for more tton a year with the management. He was neither 
give a notice nor paid in lieu of notice of one month. 


In the result, 1 hold that the demand of the Coalfield 
Labour Union that the dependent of Shri Kartik Mahato 
Watchman of Chasnalla Colliery Air Strip of Mis.l IISCO. 
who died in the year 1980 should be given employment in 
the company in terms of para 10.4,2 of NCWA-lj b not 
justified and ns such Maheshwar Pd, Mahato is not entitled 
to any relief. 

This is my Award. 

1. N. SINHA. Presiding Officer 
INo. 1-20012(32) 183-D-I1I(A)1 
A. V. S. SARMA, Desk Officer 

4^ 19 1986 


3. Ihe case of the workman furtlier b that the termination 
order was punitive, malaflde, vindictive, arbitrary and illegal 
inconlravention of provisions of Section 25F and 23H of 
the Industrial Disputes Act since it amounts to retrenchment 
within the meaning of Section 2 (OO) of the l.D. Act. 

4. The case of the management is that he was appointed 
on probation for a period of six months which may be 
extended if necessary and as per the terms of the appoint- 
ment oicer services could be terminated without notice at 
any time, tie was often found missing from place of hb 
work unauthori^dly. He was given number of warnings 
but he did not miprove. Therefore his scrsiccs were tennni- 
imied as per the terms of his service condition. As such 
ttere IS no question of provision of Section 25F and 25H 
being contravened.

8. The fact that the workman was not doing his duty 
properly and his work was iinsatisfuctory is of no avail to 
the management at least for the purpose of termination. 
Though it may be relevant for the back wages. 1, therefore, 
hold that the termination of the workman amounts to re- 
rrohehment. and his termination without complying with 
the provision of S. 25F of the J.D. Act renders the termi¬ 
nation void ab-initio. 

9. Ah far as Sec. 25H is concerned there is no evidence 
on .record that there were any person junior to the work¬ 
man. Management has also taken up a plea that it is not 
an industry which is contrary to settled law in this regard. 
I, therefore^ need not dwell upon it. 

10. For the reasons discussed above w'orkman Slui Manni- 
lal is entitled to be reinstated with effect from the date of 
his termination i.e. lS-10-1984. As far us his back wages, 
I find that he unaufhoriscdly absented himself from duty 
and his work was not satisfactory.. Therefore to my mind 
he is not entitled to back wages. Consequently T answer 
the reference as under .— 

That the action of the management of Vehicle Factory,

(In the matter of the dispute for adjudication under Sec¬ 
tion 10(1) (d) of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947 between 
the workmen an dthe Management of Food Corporation of 
India, Madras-72).

Reference: Order No. L-42012(37)l8’ ^'1I(B)D1IVB1DV. 

dated 3-4-1984 of the Ministry of I,abour and Rehabilitation 
Government of India, New Delhi. 

This dispute coming on this day foi. .inal disposal upon 
persuing the reference, claim and other concerned docu¬ 
ments on record and upon hearing of Thiruvalargal S, Kana- 
gasabai and K. Paramarap, Advocates appearing for the 
workman and the Management being absent, this Tribunal 
passed the following. 

AWARD 

This dispute between the workman Thiru Adaikkan and 
the Management of Food Corporation of India, Madras-72 
arising out of a reference under Section 10(l)(d) of the In¬ 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1947 by the Government of India in 
its Order No. L-42012(37) 83-DII(B) DIV,B(DV. dated 
3-4-1984 of the Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation for 
adjudication of the following issue; 

“Wlicther the action of the management of the Food 
Coipration of India, Madras in terminating the 
services of Shri Adaikkan, NMR workman at their 
Avadi Depot, Madras (Construction and Mainte¬ 
nance) w.e.f. 28-9-76 and not absorbing him as mes¬ 
senger [Depot) on regular basis along with hi® ju¬ 
niors w.e.f, 22-3-1979 is justified 7 If noi, to 
what relief he is entitled to.’’ 

(2) Parties were served with summons. 

(3) The Petitioner-workman Thiru Adaikkan filed hi® claim 
statement on 14-5-1986 ptjtting forth his claiiu. 

(4) Today, when the dispute was called, the Petitioner was 
present and represented by counsel. The Respondent Ma¬ 
nagement was absent and not represented and set exparte. 
Counter statement was also not filed by the Respondent- 
Management. The Petitioner was examined as MWl. 

(5) An award is passed holding the termination of the 
Petitioner as illegal and directing his reinstatement with 
wages. Continuity of service and other attendent benefits. No 
costs." 

Dated this 22nd day of August, 1986.
